# Spec §11 — Decisions made — do not re-litigate

A decision here is binding: it is not re-litigated, and a phase that needs a new one records it
here BEFORE the code (root `CLAUDE.md`). The entries were one chronological list until
2026-09-26; they are now grouped by the work that made them, in their original order within
each file. **Find a decision by its ID in the table below, then read only that file.**

**Adding one:** append it to the file for the road map in progress (a new road map gets a new
file), in the style of its neighbours: a bold one-line decision, an ID if the road map numbers
them, then the reasoning and the risk it accepts. Add its row here in the same change.
The Lines cell is `wc -l`, and `make test` checks it (`tools/proof/spec_index_proof.py`).

| G-32 | `paste_attributes` takes a fifth group, `graphic`: a clip's props copied whole onto clips drawn by the same module, the target keeping its module, size and timeline; never in the default set (⌥⌘V is unchanged); `skip_missing` skips what cannot take a named group instead of refusing; the copied map is read only when a target takes it; Edit ▸ Paste Graphic Properties is the person's door; amends G-10's paste sentence; (e) amended by G-34: a target key an action on the target names is kept | [`graphics.md`](graphics.md) |
| G-33 | On an export row that needs even pixel dimensions the core resolves and lists the even size the file will have — an odd edge one pixel smaller, the Document row named "Document (even)" — so the sheet and the `export` tool report the file's size and no size of the canvas's own shape is refused for being odd; `hevc_alpha`, "HEVC with Alpha (MOV)", is a tenth row whose frames carry the canvas's alpha; every movie row fills the frame instead of padding it when the miss is within a pixel or the frame is a canvas-shaped size less a pixel (one integer test, shared with the alpha guard), and letterboxes or, on an alpha row, refuses past that; a missing encoder is the preset's own sentence and no file; `RC_FORMAT_VERSION` stays 11; amended by #243: the export sheet 60 pt wider and its footnote on a line of its own above the buttons, so every footnote measured, the default rows and the longest two, is drawn whole; amended by G-35: the two alpha movie rows are written straight and labelled so, and Frame Job reads a video frame's declared alpha mode | [`graphics.md`](graphics.md) |
| G-34 | A graphic's props are action targets: a number and a colour (in OKLab, straight alpha — amended by G-40) ramp, a string, boolean or enum switches at the end, an asset switches at the end (G-42), and a prop its module marks `animate: false` (G-41) is refused; the kind comes from the schema the op carries and is stored in the end's `kinds` (a caller's `kinds` is discarded); a named prop is range-checked, a stored end never re-checked; a prop the clip lacks starts from the schema's current default, written in the same step, and a paste keeps an animated start (amends G-32 (e)); numbers arrive fractional; `RC_FORMAT_VERSION` 13; supersedes G-10's first sentence; its string field's 170 pt cap amended by G-48 | [`graphics.md`](graphics.md) |
| G-35 | A video frame is composited as premultiplied only when its buffer's alpha-mode attachment says `PremultipliedAlpha`, an unlabelled one as straight; `prores_4444` and `hevc_alpha` are written straight — each frame un-premultiplied in integers (exact for 8 bits), each transparent pixel within 4 px of a visible one filled with the nearest one's colour, every buffer labelled `StraightAlpha` and `hevc_alpha`'s encoder key straight; `RC_FORMAT_VERSION` unchanged, no migration (the owner's "do both", #242); amends G-33 (d) and (h); amended by PR-3 (`prores_4444` un-premultiplied in float, `hevc_alpha` rounded to 8 bits first) — amended by CX-17 (`vp9_alpha_webm` straightened in float too) | [`graphics.md`](graphics.md) |
| G-36 | Playback rasterizes upcoming graphic frames ahead on worker threads too, on the document's key while playing forward with a graphic clip; a session starts at the seek target or the playhead on the composition's grid and restarts on play, seek, item swap and a property edit while playing; workers follow the compositor's reached frame, counting `skipped`; G-26's bounds per session until #127's next phase replaces them with 25 % of physical memory (the owner's choice: no fixed limit) and a setting for drawing ahead while paused; the compositor stays the one consumer and the texture key decides; amends G-26 (a) — amended by G-37 — amended by G-38 | [`graphics.md`](graphics.md) |
| G-37 | Graphic rasters share one memory budget, a quarter of physical memory, over every session's held rasters and the kept frames, under 1,024 entries per session; memory pressure halves it (warning) or zeroes it (critical); a document's playback keeps the graphic frames it drew under their texture keys, served on a pool miss before any slot and dropped when the window closes; `FrameJobGraphicKeepOff` keeps nothing; amends G-26 (d) and G-36 (e) — amended by G-38 — amended by G-39 | [`graphics.md`](graphics.md) |
| G-38 | Graphic frames are drawn ahead while paused once the person has left Frame Job alone (Settings ▸ Playback ▸ Background render, on after 5 s by default, as Final Cut's; a person's setting with no tool): any key, click, drag, scroll or gesture, any edit and any transport move restarts the clock and stops the pass; the document whose transport last moved, paused, built and drawing a graphic, draws from its playhead at utility QoS into its kept frames, marked as drawn ahead, which give way last and which a play from the pass's start does not push out; one pass at a time, never beside any window's play, Frame Job opted out of App Nap for its life; ends `done`, `full` or `stopped`; amends G-36 (a) and G-37 (c). Measured on parive-promo's `section` scene (Release, Full, 10 s from tick 25 120 000, 12 cores, 96 GiB, `powermode 0`, 2026-10-21): 0.292 of its frames composed with Background render off (load average 31.8 before the play), 1.0 after a `full` pass (3,103 rasters, 25.8 GB kept; load average 87.1; measured on the code before fix pass 1, whose bench was cut short by host activity) | [`graphics.md`](graphics.md) |
| G-39 | A graphic's PNG and JPEG assets are kept as stored PNG copies that decode to the same pixmap, in one process-wide cache keyed by content and sized from G-37's budget: the straight RGBA8 written as a stored, unfiltered RGBA PNG on the first frame that draws it, used only when it decodes to the original's pixmap at the original's size; keyed by the SHA-256 of the asset's bytes; its limit `budgetBytes / 16` at the level in force (physical/64 at normal, physical/128 at warning, 0 at critical), trimmed least recently used first; a copy that does not fit is remembered by its size and drawn the original way until there is room, so nothing is rebuilt per frame; `FrameJobGraphicImageCacheOff` sets 0; amends G-37. Measured on parive-promo's `section` scene (40 frames of 1920 × 1080, 5 rounds, load average 6.10, `powermode 0`, 2026-10-22): a saving of 0.0429 against a spread of 0.0330 | [`graphics.md`](graphics.md) |
| G-40 | An action on a graphic `color` prop blends in OKLab, as stdlib's `interpolateColors` does, with straight alpha; no setting and no per-action or per-end key; one fixture, `graphic/tests/oklab.json`, both answer; no format bump, no migration; amends G-34 (f) — amended by G-43 | [`graphics.md`](graphics.md) |
| G-41 | A schema entry's optional boolean `animate: false` (any type) means no action animates the prop: `set_action_end` refuses a non-null value for it by name, after an unknown key, a `null` still removing; a non-boolean `animate` is refused by the engine and the core in one sentence; a stored end is not re-checked, and the Graphic tab lists such a key as the end's stale row; with an action selected the row is ⊘ and writes `set_graphic`; no format bump; amends G-34 (b), (l) and its accepted limits | [`graphics.md`](graphics.md) |

| G-43 | Every colour an action animates blends in OKLab with straight alpha, by key: the nine `[r, g, b, a]` / `video.chroma.key` paths in `video` and `text`, a key whose ends are equal kept exact, inputs clamped to [0, 1], in floating point; one implementation, `model_color.rs`'s unit path, which `oklab_mix` rounds; `oklab.json` gains a third reader; no format bump; amends G-40 (d), G-34 (f) | [`graphics.md`](graphics.md) |

| PUB-1 | A build for another person's Mac is made only by GitHub Actions on a push to `publish`: a Developer ID-signed, notarized, stapled DMG on a GitHub Release; local builds stay ad-hoc signed — amended by NM-3 — amended by PUB-6 | [`publishing.md`](publishing.md) |
| PUB-2 | The version is `<MAJOR.MINOR>.<run number>`, the build number is the run number, and the base is the `VERSION` file, as in Rasterize | [`publishing.md`](publishing.md) |
| PUB-3 | What is published is the Distribution configuration, archived and then exported with the `developer-id` method | [`publishing.md`](publishing.md) |
| PUB-4 | The exported app is checked by `published_check.py` before it is packed, and a failed row publishes nothing | [`publishing.md`](publishing.md) |
| PUB-5 | The DMG is what is notarized and stapled, with an App Store Connect API key | [`publishing.md`](publishing.md) |
| PUB-6 | Sparkle 2 updates the published build from `appcast.xml` on the latest GitHub Release, beside `FrameJob.dmg`, one item per release; amends PUB-1's "no updater"; the repository goes public first; the feed URL is fixed; Latest is always the highest run number (`--latest=false` for an older re-run) | [`publishing.md`](publishing.md) |
| PUB-7 | Only Distribution holds an updater (`FRAMEJOB_UPDATER`, the feed and the key); Sparkle.framework is embedded in every configuration and loaded only in Distribution, so a local ad-hoc Distribution build cannot be launched; Check for Updates… has no MCP mirror | [`publishing.md`](publishing.md) |
| PUB-8 | The owner generates the EdDSA key; the private key is the `publish` environment's `SPARKLE_ED_PRIVATE_KEY`, the public key in `project.yml`; the workflow fails without the secret, D30/D31 and P19/P20 refuse an empty feed or key; the stapled DMG is what is signed | [`publishing.md`](publishing.md) |
| PUB-9 | Sparkle's own defaults: it asks on the second launch, a daily check, no automatic install, no deltas, release notes only as a link to the release page | [`publishing.md`](publishing.md) |
| PUB-10 | Every configuration carries `Contents/Resources/Acknowledgements.txt`, written each build by `tools/licenses/acknowledgements.py`: crates over every non-dev edge from `cargo metadata`, npm from `agent/bun.lock`, root licence files or the canonical SPDX text under the authors, `NESTED` for nested licence files, Sparkle's own `LICENSE`, Bun's vendored at `BUN_LICENSE_VERSION` (`--strict-bun` in Distribution), the pins, the model and the fonts; amends CX-5 with `vector.c-LICENSE`; no About-panel entry; D42/D43 check it with their own readers | [`publishing.md`](publishing.md) |
| PUB-11 | `published_check.py` carries P22–P25: P22 each encoder's own string literal in the binary's bytes (the archive strips D37's symbol table), P23 = D39, P24 = D41, P25 = D43; no P row reads `nm` | [`publishing.md`](publishing.md) |
| PUB-12 | The workflow restores `build/codecs` from the Actions cache under `codecs-<build_codecs.py --print-key>`, computed under "Build Codecs"' PATH prefix, no `restore-keys`; after Archive a stamp check warns on a missing stamp or another key and then never saves; a miss with equal keys saves even when a later step fails; the stamp still decides reuse; setup-bun installs `BUN_LICENSE_VERSION` | [`publishing.md`](publishing.md) |
| MT-1 | A matte is a track relation in a closed vocabulary (`none`, `alpha`, `alpha_inverted`, `luma`, `luma_inverted`), set by `set_track {matte}`, drawn by the one compositor; a matte track's clips are never drawn and any clip that draws can be one; `tracks[].matte` written only when not `none`, format 14, no migrate step | [`mattes.md`](mattes.md) |
| MT-2 | A matte cuts the track directly behind it in its own container unless that track is a matte track; with no target it cuts nothing and still draws nothing; where it shows nothing the track behind shows uncut; read per instant, no clip names another; its clips still sound | [`mattes.md`](mattes.md) |
| MT-3 | `alpha`, `alpha_inverted`, Rec. 709 `luma` of the premultiplied colour and `luma_inverted`, in encoded sRGB, multiply the matted layer's coverage at step 7 at its own canvas position — so its shadow and its reflection are cut where they land — and the outgoing half of a dissolve multiplies its partner's cover by the same value; `TrackMatte` is a shader discriminant, append only | [`mattes.md`](mattes.md) |
| MT-4 | A matte clip's placement, crop, colour, filters, motion, actions and transitions shape the matte; its blend mode, drop shadow and reflection do nothing, the core emitting `normal`, `null`, `null` (the recipe's second deliberate override); amends G-10 for a graphic on a matte track | [`mattes.md`](mattes.md) |
| MT-5 | In nests the first matte level walking outward decides where a layer draws, every inner target level cuts it; a matte is keyed by (enclosing nest-clip path, matte track id); `nest` and `unnest` count from the front-most non-matte track and carry a matte only with its target | [`mattes.md`](mattes.md) |
| MT-6 | Each listed matte is drawn into a destination-sized `bgra8Unorm` target before the destination pass, each set of mattes resolved into one `r8Unorm` coverage sampled at `texture(1)`; an unmatted layer binds the 1×1 white placeholder there (MT0's spike); `LayerUniforms` stays 104 floats; amended by PR-1 (`rgba16Float`) | [`mattes.md`](mattes.md) |
| MT-7 | Set by Track ▸ Matte and the track context menu's Matte submenu (Off, Alpha, Inverted Alpha, Luma, Inverted Luma), undo "Track Matte", no toggle or shortcut; the header's tag in the label colour on an accent tint ("Matte · <mode>", dimmed "Matte · nothing behind"), the lane at half opacity with dashed outlines and a bracket to its target, the Clip tab's note, a matte clip the topmost canvas hit | [`mattes.md`](mattes.md) |
| MT-8 | A `cross_dissolve` or `zoom` whose container is a nest, or one of whose clips is a nest clip, is ROUTED: each half drawn with no dissolve weight into its own destination-sized target, then one full-frame pass mixes them (1 − p)·out + p·in, a zoom's halves scaled there about their anchors; the core decides it (the layer's `dissolve`, never its `transition`; `normal`); a matte layer keeps only the dissolves at or inside its first matte level; a timeline pair of non-nest clips keeps MT-3's correction; push, slide, wipe and the fades are not routed; amended by PR-1 (`rgba16Float`) | [`mattes.md`](mattes.md) |
| MT-9 | A frame's mattes are planned first (`MattePlan`): each cut set resolved once its last matte is drawn, a matte's composite given back to the pool once every cut set naming it is resolved; the pool keeps, of each kind, the most any of the renderer's last 30 frames held (a frame is one `render` call) and releases the rest; no cap, a target Metal cannot make fails the frame; amends MT-6's pooling sentence; amended by PR-1 (`rgba16Float`) | [`mattes.md`](mattes.md) |
| PR-1 | The working depth is 16-bit float holding encoded values clamped to [0, 1]: `64RGBAHalf` render-context buffers, `rgba16Float` destination, matte composites, routed dissolve halves, shadow pair and layer, blend, shadow and dissolve pipeline attachments; the coverage stays `r8Unorm` and the placeholder `bgra8Unorm`; the source sample, blend and dissolve `saturate`d; blending in encoded sRGB; the preview shows the same buffers, no EDR or HDR; amends MT-6, MT-8 and MT-9's figures; the measured memory (heavy 4K export +851.7 MB) accepted by the owner | [`precision.md`](precision.md) |
| PR-2 | Every video source decodes to `64RGBAHalf`, wrapped as `rgba16Float`; #216's 32BGRA import check stands; HDR stays tone-mapped (`supportsHDRSourceFrames` false) — amended by GR-15 | [`precision.md`](precision.md) |
| PR-3 | The export reader's frames are `64RGBAHalf`; `prores_422` and `prores_4444` take them as they are; a new `hevc_10bit` row (Main 10, after `hevc_alpha`, even-sized, no alpha, HEVC's tiers); `hevc` pinned to Main; `h264` and `hevc` rounded to 8-bit BGRA first; amends G-35 (c): `prores_4444` un-premultiplied in place in float, `hevc_alpha` rounded to 8-bit premultiplied BGRA then straightened unchanged | [`precision.md`](precision.md) |
| PR-4 | `render_frame`, Save Frame…, GIF and APNG frames are the generator's CGImage: 8-bit when every source is 8-bit, 16-bit float when any is deeper; today's behaviour written down; no `depth` argument; JPEG stays 8-bit — amended by PR-6: "any source is deeper" counts video sources only, as P2-1 measured: a project whose only picture is a deep still renders 8-bit (S4, accepted by the owner), as does a deep still over 8-bit video (S5); over 10-bit video the frame is 16-bit and keeps the still's depth (S6) — amended by PR-7: a reversed clip counts by its 10-bit intermediate, so a project whose only video is a reversed 8-bit clip renders 16-bit (V6, accepted by the owner) — amended by PR-9: a frame whose recipe draws a deep still is read through export's reader (`render_frame`, Save Frame…): a 16-bit PNG, a 10-bit HEIC; GIF, APNG and JPEG stay 8-bit — amended by PR-11 — amended by PR-12: a frame showing a deep VP9 track is read through the reader too — amended by HD-2 — amended by PR-14: a frame the generator answers 8-bit while it shows a deeper source is re-read | [`precision.md`](precision.md) |

| PR-13 | A graphic's gradients (linear and radial, two-stop and many-stop) are dithered where tiny-skia rounds them to 8 bits: tiny-skia 0.12.0 vendored in `graphic/vendor/` with a four-file patch (`Stage::Dither`, last in `Gradient::push_stages`, highp only), linked by `[patch.crates-io]` in both `graphic/` and `core/`; a static offset by pixel position (lowbias32, salted seed), uniform over ±7/16 of a level, one offset for R, G and B clamped to [0, a], alpha untouched, so a colour on a level stays exact; solid fills, images and text untouched; anchored to the pixmap drawn into; limit: an 8-bit lossy export (`h264`, `hevc`, at default quality or 8/16 Mbit/s) strips it and a wider span does not help, so a delivery file keeps a gradient smooth only at 10 bits (`hevc_10bit`, later #377) or ProRes; no op, FFI, catalog, schema or format change; amends PR-5 (owner, 2026-10-27, #375) | [`precision.md`](precision.md) |

| SY-39 | Keep pitch at 0.999 ≤ speed ≤ 1.001 (≠ 1) plays `off` (varispeed; ≤ 1.74 cents), amending SY-26's band to 0.98–1.02 outside it; ×1 `spectral`; names unchanged, preview and export share it; R3 pinned at 1.0 ms, E35 measured, M16–M18 | [`audio-sync-3.md`](audio-sync-3.md) |

| TR-1 | Fillers come from AssemblyAI's `disfluencies: true` on every cloud request from CT-1 on; the marker records it (amends R6-15); the caption producers never see a filler, so captions stay byte-identical; road map 6's refusal superseded | [`cut-by-transcript.md`](cut-by-transcript.md) |
| TR-2 | A transcript cached before CT-1 stays current for captions; a filler run needs a `disfluencies` marker, and the re-transcribe is the person's ask on that media after the `CloudEstimate` line | [`cut-by-transcript.md`](cut-by-transcript.md) |
| TR-3 | Two paths, split by who judges: fillers are deterministic (a closed vocabulary on its own timing), false starts a judgement of meaning — amended by TX-70 | [`cut-by-transcript.md`](cut-by-transcript.md) |
| TR-4 | Remove Filler Words… is shaped like Remove Silences: a sheet, a candidate wash, one `ripple_delete_ranges` undo step, the read-half tool `detect_fillers`; edges on the 32 ms VAD grid, frame-snapped; no new op, no format change | [`cut-by-transcript.md`](cut-by-transcript.md) |
| TR-5 | False starts go to the assistant under AI-6 (amended): on the person's ask it chooses words to cut, read through the app-state tool `get_transcript` (a clip's words in timeline times, never fetched over the network; AI-7 unchanged), proposes, then makes ordinary undoable cuts | [`cut-by-transcript.md`](cut-by-transcript.md) |
| TR-6 | The working arrangement: the `transcript-cut` worktree, each phase gated, committed and merged into main alone; synthetic fixtures and the fake AssemblyAI, paid calls only behind their flags and budgets; owner media read in place, never committed or copied; agents list the `CLAUDE.md` edits owed | [`cut-by-transcript.md`](cut-by-transcript.md) |
| TR-7 | Filler removal is cloud-only: on the owner's scripted sample the on-device engine kept 1 of 8 fillers (98 % of other words recognised), so it is not a filler source; media with no `.fillers` transcript get the sheet's note and the estimated re-transcribe; on-device captions unchanged | [`cut-by-transcript.md`](cut-by-transcript.md) |
| TR-8 | The caption filler vocabulary: um, uh, hmm, mhm, uh-huh, ah, huh, hm, m, er, erm, matched lowercased with edge punctuation trimmed; its sources as read on 2026-10-22, none published for Universal-3.5 Pro; captions drop exactly these, the cache keeps every word | [`cut-by-transcript.md`](cut-by-transcript.md) |
| TR-9 | Filler cut edges: a run of consecutive fillers, widened outward to the 32 ms Silero grid but never past the neighbouring words, then snapped inward to frames; a locked-track clip gives none; a candidate over another unlocked clip's word, a nest or a transition wall is dropped whole | [`cut-by-transcript.md`](cut-by-transcript.md) |
| TR-10 | Clip ▸ Remove Filler Words…: no tuning controls, `detect_fillers`' opening scope, one `ripple_delete_ranges` step named "Remove Filler Words", the `candidateSpans` wash, after Remove Silences… with no key; its Transcribe re-transcribes one media with that media's previous request, the shipped models, `disfluencies` and `refresh`, priced by `CloudEstimate.describe` and introduced by R6-2's upload sentence; Remove disabled during a run; no captions, no undo step; the mirror `transcribe_for_fillers {media}`, off main, always withheld from the assistant (amends AI-7) — amended by CJ-12 | [`cut-by-transcript.md`](cut-by-transcript.md) |
| TR-11 | `get_transcript {clip, language?}`: one clip's words in timeline ticks, sorted, each with TR-8's `filler` flag, and `fillers_reported`; from the media's cached transcript when it serves `.captions`, else this Mac's recogniser over the clip, never the network, no cache written; off main, its on-device run reached by the assistant's Stop (amends AI-16); no AI-7 row; the prompt's false-start paragraph (read the words, say which, one `ripple_delete_ranges`) — amended by TX-33 | [`cut-by-transcript.md`](cut-by-transcript.md) |
| TR-12 | An agent's `ripple_delete_ranges` names its undo step "Ripple Delete" (⌘⌫'s and the singular tools' name), whatever it cuts; no caller-passed name; the sheets keep "Remove Silences" and "Remove Filler Words" (TR-10) | [`cut-by-transcript.md`](cut-by-transcript.md) |
| TR-13 | `get_transcript`'s optional `from`/`to`: only the words that overlap the timeline window, snapped and clamped to the clip, a word across an edge answered whole; refused when empty or outside the clip, after the jobs refusal and before the spelling; `from`/`to` echoed; an on-device run hears the window and 2 s of source either side; one overlap filter for both engines (#332) | [`cut-by-transcript.md`](cut-by-transcript.md) |
| TR-14 | `get_transcript`'s on-device words cached in `cache/device-transcripts/<media>-<language>.json`, keyed by R6-15's source stamp, the language, the OS version and a version; a call whose span is inside a stored run answers from it (`cached` true), a miss runs its span and the store prunes the runs it contains; stored as media source ticks, mapped by `MediaTranscript.project`; read by no other caller — amended by TX-12 (the timeline transcript reads it too, one whole-file run per media); nothing stored on a throw, a cancel or a changed file — amended by TX-16 | [`cut-by-transcript.md`](cut-by-transcript.md) |
